# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

## Envelope

* `[✅]`   `envelope/possession_statement` **Pairing-typed proof-of-possession statements for first- and second-group envelope keys and commitments, with canonical descriptions that flatten them to precompile encodings and validate decoded fields; creates the `adapters/envelope` crate**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: each envelope key is registered with a Schnorr proof of possession the contract verifies, so the proof's challenge is a scalar the client and the contract recompute from one byte form; a Fiat–Shamir challenge is computed over the full public statement and the first message, which for a proof of possession are the key and the commitment, both group elements; and every message hashed to a scalar passes through the one encoding, so the statement is a value with a canonical field sequence the encoding factory encodes (LC-08; CR-09's Fiat–Shamir form; `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`'s Buyer keys statement; `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`'s proof system; the product requirements' canonical encoding position)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: distinct `PossessionG1Statement<P>` and `PossessionG2Statement<P>` hold the key and commitment as `P::G1` or `P::G2` for one selected `P: IPairingAdapter`; neither trusted type stores arbitrary bytes
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the statement's description states its canonical fields as a byte string followed by a byte string, maps a statement to its canonical field values, and maps canonical field values back to a statement or to the refusal that names what failed
    * `[✅]`   Functional: reconstruction reads both fields through the encoding family's canonical field reader, carrying its refusal whole, then validates each encoded group element through the selected pairing's `decode_g1` or `decode_g2`, returning a typed refusal at the first invalid encoding
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the reference statement maps to the reference fields and the reference fields map back to the reference statement
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the crate depends on `adapters/encoding` and `adapters/pairing`; no curve, hash, format, or vendor is named

    * `[✅]`   The description flattens typed group elements through the selected pairing's `encode_g1` or `encode_g2` at the canonical-field boundary
    * `[✅]`   Does not produce the statement's bytes or hash them; the encoding factory encodes the statement and the hash-to-scalar family maps the encoding to the challenge under the tag `envelope/pairing_elgamal` holds

* `[✅]`   `envelope/pairing_elgamal` **Pairing ElGamal key-agreement concrete over any pairing concrete: key-pair generation from caller-supplied secret draws with a Schnorr proof of possession per key over the encoded possession statement, the wrap of a credential's components to a recipient's public keys under coins drawn from the randomness family, the unwrap under the recipient's key pair, and fallible constructors rebuilding every value from its pairing components, a recipient's public keys admitted only with their proof of possession; authors the key-agreement family's generic interface, identifier, algebra declaration, coins, components, and mock**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: a credential is delivered to one identity by encrypting its components `(A, B)` to that identity's registered envelope keys `pk1 = g1^x` and `pk2 = g2^y`, whose secrets are independent and nonzero, each key registered with a Schnorr proof of possession and neither the identity element; the envelope is `(g1^ρ, A · pk1^ρ, g2^σ, B · pk2^σ)` under independent coins, and the delivery proof takes `ρ`, `σ`, and the seller's `(x, y)` as witnesses; the construction passes through one repo-owned interface, `wrapTo(recipientPubkey, plaintext)` and `unwrap(recipientPrivkey, cyphertext)`, whose concrete owns its key-pair and envelope types as associated types with their fallible constructors (CR-04; LC-08 at the algebra level; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Key Agreement statement; `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`'s Buyer keys and Envelope statements; the dependency map's `envelope/pairing_elgamal` row; the Envelope milestone)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: key generation samples `x` and `y` from caller-supplied uniform bytes through the pairing scalar type's sampling bound, forms `pk1 = x·g1` and `pk2 = y·g2`, refuses a key that is the identity element of its group and a secret shared across the groups, detected as `e(pk1, g2) = e(g1, pk2)`, and returns the key pair
    * `[✅]`   Functional: key generation returns, for each key, a Schnorr proof of possession `(R, z)` with `R = k·g` for a nonce `k` sampled from bytes the injected randomness source draws, `c` the hash-to-scalar family's mapping, under the key's group's domain tag, of the encoding factory's encoding of the possession statement holding the key's and `R`'s precompile encodings, and `z = k + c·secret`
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the wrap takes a recipient's public keys and the credential KEM's `CredentialComponents` `(A, B)`, samples `ρ` and `σ` from separate draws of the injected randomness source, and returns the envelope `(C1, C2, D1, D2) = (ρ·g1, A + ρ·pk1, σ·g2, B + σ·pk2)` with `ρ` and `σ` inside `Secret`s, the delivery proof's witnesses
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the unwrap returns the `CredentialComponents` `(A, B) = (C2 − x·C1, D2 − y·D1)` under the recipient's key pair, so every envelope wrapped to a key pair's public keys opens under that key pair to the components it wrapped, and opens under any other key pair, or with an element taken from another envelope, to different components, which the credential KEM's `is_valid` refuses
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every concrete-owned type is rebuilt from its components through a fallible constructor: the key pair's refuses an identity key and a secret shared across the groups; the public keys' refuses the same and a key whose proof of possession does not satisfy `z·g = R + c·pk`, so every public keys value a sender wraps to has passed LC-08's registration hygiene; the proof of possession's and the envelope's error arms are uninhabited
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every concrete declares the key-agreement identifier a deployment's hash-card names, the envelope algebra a delivery-proof concrete admits it by, the domain tag each group's proof of possession hashes its challenge under, its adapter version, and the interface version it implements, readable before any instance exists, so a consumer that mirrors the proofs of possession reads the tags from the declaration and names no concrete
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the family's interface names the pairing concrete a key-agreement adapter computes over, so a consumer that holds that pairing receives the envelope's values in the pairing's own scalar and group types and combines them with the credential KEM's and the delivery proof's
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the key pair is exposed as `(x, y)` inside `Secret`s, so custody persists it and the transfer proof takes it as a witness, and as its public keys `(pk1, pk2)`, so the identity workflow registers them and the delivery proof states them; a recipient's public keys as `(pk1, pk2)`; the proof of possession as `(R1, z1, R2, z2)`, so the registry verifies it; and the envelope as `(C1, C2, D1, D2)`, so the settlement carries it, the delivery proof states it, and a holder rebuilds it from chain history

## Delivery proof and on-chain verifier

* `[✅]`   `domain/signature_scheme_identifier` **Signature scheme identifier, the `uint8` the identity binding and the claim verifier's key record store and every signature concrete's declaration carries, held as a distinct type reachable without a signature crate, admitted only when it can name a declared scheme**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the identity binding stores the bound handshake key's scheme identifier and the claim verifier's key record stores the attestation key's scheme identifier, each `scheme uint8`, and the chain family's identity form derives an address over a public key's canonical bytes and its scheme identifier with no signature dependency, so the identifier is a type both the signature family and the chain family reach from the domain ring, and the zero value an unassigned slot reads as is refused (the technical requirements' `Binding` and `VerifierKey` schemas and signature family row; the dependency map's `domain/signature_scheme_identifier` and `chain/evm_forms` rows and its address derivation statement)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: one type holds the scheme identifier's `u8`, reachable only through a read accessor, and its only producer is a fallible constructor
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the constructor refuses `0`, the value an unassigned `uint8` storage slot reads as, so it names no declared scheme
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every other `u8` is admitted and read back unchanged
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the module depends on the standard library alone; the `domain` crate's dependencies are unchanged

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   Domain: an owned value type in the protocol and domain ring, the scheme every signature declaration, identity binding, verifier key record, and address derivation names
    * `[✅]`   Does not assign scheme identifiers or map one to a signature concrete; each signature concrete's declaration carries its own, and the adapter registry binds the concrete per identifier
    * `[✅]`   Does not derive an address or interpret a public key
    * `[✅]`   Does not decode the identifier from wire bytes; the encoding family decodes a `uint8` into the `u8` this constructor takes
    * `[✅]`   Does not create any other module of the `domain` crate
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `module`
    * `[✅]`   Bounded context: the `signature_scheme_identifier` module of the `domain` crate, holding `SignatureSchemeIdentifier`, its constructor params, and its constructor's error and return types

    * `[✅]`   Outside: signing, verification, key encodings, address derivation, the registry binding, and the encoding of the identifier on the wire

* `[✅]`   `chain/evm_forms` **The EVM suite's forms as the chain family's first concrete: the identity, entitlement, interval, and chain-identifier forms, the key identifier the identity form derives from a public key and a scheme identifier, and the signed-intent form that returns the EIP-712 digest a signature covers, constructed over a borrowed encoder whose type names it reads; consumes the encoding family's canonical-field contract; authors the chain family's form interface, its declaration, and their mocks; creates the `adapters/chain` crate**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the delivery proof's challenge is the hash-to-scalar mapping of the transcript's canonical encoding, which the contract recomputes, so a transcript's chain, entitlement, interval, and identity fields must take exactly the forms the hash-card's forms identifier names; the forms are the suite's, fixed by the suite and carried explicitly so no client derives them from application state and two client versions never encode one deployment differently; the EVM suite's schemas fix an identity, the identity's contract account and the entitlement contract alike, as a twenty-byte address, an entitlement as an ERC-721 token's 256-bit unsigned identifier, an interval counter as a 64-bit unsigned value, and a chain identifier as a 256-bit unsigned value; the same suite keys an identity account's signer set by a scheme-tagged 32-byte key identifier and accepts an identity's action only as a signed intent bound to a chain, a contract, an identity, a nonce, and an expiry; so the EVM suite needs one concrete supplying those forms, each yielding and rebuilt from one canonical field, the key identifier derived the same way for every scheme, and the signed intent produced as the EIP-712 digest whose member types are the names the encoder in use returns, beneath a declaration naming its forms identifier and the encoding identifier it requires, which the family-owned function `chain/create_chain_forms` admits against the identifiers the suite requires (CR-09; LC-13; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s transcript per delivery-statement version, Signatures, and the hash-card's Forms identifier field; the technical requirements' `Entitlement`, `Binding`, `Deployment`, `IntentNonce`, and `DeviceSigner` schemas; the product requirements' contract-suite position; the dependency map's `chain/evm_forms` row; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the forms portion of the chain family's declaration names the forms identifier a deployment's hash-card names, the adapter version, the interface version the concrete implements, the encoding identifier the concrete requires, the domain tag of the key identifier, the name, version, and struct name of the signed intent's domain, the names of the intent's members, and the canonical kinds of its nonce and expiry, readable before any instance exists
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the identity form holds twenty bytes, declares the fixed twenty-byte kind, yields the fixed twenty-byte field of its bytes, is rebuilt from a fixed twenty-byte field, and refuses a field of any other kind, naming the kind it expected
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the entitlement form holds a 256-bit unsigned value as 32 big-endian bytes, declares the 256-bit unsigned kind, yields the 256-bit unsigned field of its bytes, is rebuilt from a 256-bit unsigned field, and refuses a field of any other kind, naming the kind it expected
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the interval form holds a 64-bit unsigned value, declares the 64-bit unsigned kind, yields the 64-bit unsigned field of its value, is rebuilt from a 64-bit unsigned field, and refuses a field of any other kind, naming the kind it expected
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the chain-identifier form holds a 256-bit unsigned value as 32 big-endian bytes, declares the 256-bit unsigned kind, yields the 256-bit unsigned field of its bytes, is rebuilt from a 256-bit unsigned field, and refuses a field of any other kind, naming the kind it expected
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the key-identifier form holds 32 bytes, declares the fixed 32-byte kind, yields the fixed 32-byte field of its bytes, is rebuilt from a fixed 32-byte field, and refuses a field of any other kind, naming the kind it expected
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the signed-intent form holds the 32-byte digest, declares the fixed 32-byte kind, yields the fixed 32-byte field of its bytes, is rebuilt from a fixed 32-byte field, and refuses a field of any other kind, naming the kind it expected
    * `[✅]`   Functional: one type implements the chain family's form interface, naming the identity, entitlement, interval, chain-identifier, and signed-intent forms above as its identity, entitlement, interval, chain-identifier, and signed-intent forms
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every form rebuilt from the field it yields equals the form that yielded it
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the identity form derives a key identifier from a public key's canonical bytes and a signature scheme identifier as the keccak256 digest of the declared domain tag, the scheme identifier's one byte, and the key's bytes in that order, so one key under one scheme always yields one key identifier, the same key under another scheme yields another, and the rule is the same for every scheme
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the forms instance produces the signed intent from a chain, a contract, an acting identity, a nonce, an expiry, and a call, an operation name with named canonical field values, as the digest of the EIP-712 typed data whose domain holds the declared name and version, the chain's identifier as `chainId`, and the contract as `verifyingContract`, and whose struct, named by the declaration, holds the acting identity, the nonce, the expiry, and a nested struct named by the operation whose members are the call's named fields in order, each member typed by the name the borrowed encoder returns for the member's canonical kind, so the output changes with each of those inputs
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the signed intent refuses an operation name that is not an identifier or that is the intent's struct name, a field name that is not an identifier or repeats an earlier field's, and a nonce or expiry whose kind is not the declared kind, each refusal naming what it refused
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the chain family's form interface names the identity, entitlement, interval, and chain-identifier forms, each bounded by the canonical-field contract, the identity form bounded by the family's identity-form interface that derives the key identifier into the key-identifier form its associated type names, and the signed-intent form, bounded by the canonical-field contract, which the family's signed-intent operation returns; it carries the family's declaration, readable before any instance exists
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: nothing in the interface names a vendor, a chain client, a curve, a hash, a byte format, a type name, or a selector; the chain crate depends on `adapters/encoding` and `crates/domain` and on no signature crate; the concrete is private to the crate

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   Adapter: the chain family's first concrete, private beneath the family's `factory` module, and the first implementation of the form interface; this node authors the concrete's fixed declaration value and the values the declaration carries
    * `[✅]`   First source file that requires the chain family's form interface, its identity-form interface, and its declaration, which it authors with their mocks, because the chain's forms are the form interface's associated types, address derivation is the identity form's operation, and the signed intent is the forms instance's operation
    * `[✅]`   Consumes the canonical-field contract `ICanonicalField` and the encoder's type-name operation, which `encoding/abi` authors; authors nothing in the encoding crate
    * `[✅]`   Constructed over a borrowed encoder obtained through the encoding factory's surface: it reads, once at construction, the type name the encoder returns for each canonical field kind and holds the names, so it holds no table of its own and names no encoding concrete
    * `[✅]`   Derives no identity address: the identity's own twenty-byte address is its account contract's, created at binding, and is a value of the identity form; the key identifier is the scheme-tagged `DeviceSigner.keyId bytes32` the account's signer set is keyed by, derived the same way for Ed25519 and secp256k1, since the account verifies each signer through the signature adapter the adapter registry binds for its scheme
    * `[✅]`   Does not sign or verify; the signature family signs the digest this concrete returns and the account contract verifies it; does not name an operation, a contract function, or a selector, whose vocabulary belongs to the contract and chain nodes that first consume it
    * `[✅]`   Does not create the forms function, its deps, params, payload, consumer, or return types, their mocks, or the family's integration test; those are `chain/create_chain_forms`'s
    * `[✅]`   Does not map the forms identifier to the hash-card's `formsId` byte; the hash-card's own encoding does that
    * `[✅]`   Does not convert a form to or from a chain client's address or integer types; `chain/base` binds the forms the function resolves
    * `[✅]`   Does not encode a transcript; the encoding factory encodes a transcript through its description, which reads each form through the canonical-field contract
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: a value a chain member's declaration fixes is known before the member is constructed, so a caller that holds the identifier configuration named reads the declaration through one family-owned function and never restates a member's declared value, forms identifier, or precompile encodings from its own knowledge of the member, and a caller outside the crate, which cannot name a member's declaration constant, reads it here alone
      * `[✅]`   Sources: the dependency map's `chain/lookup_chain_declaration` row, "No caller restates a chain member's declared value", and its `chain/base ownership` list, "a value the chain fixes is read before construction through `chain/lookup_chain_declaration`"; the technical requirements' Chain family ownership list, "the declaration lookup `chain/lookup_chain_declaration`"; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope entry for `chain/lookup_chain_declaration`; the Concrete Couplings Handoff's problem "the chain's network values" and the `harness-crypto/run` entry, "the precompile encodings handed to `create_pairing` are read through `chain/lookup_chain_declaration` from the configured chain selection's identifier"; the product requirements' Resolved Positions on the harness being configured and on the adapter family form; `families.md`, "What a family owns", "Reading a declaration before construction", and "Ownership at the first consumer"; `composition.md`, "Nested calls build their inputs from what they receive (Rust)"
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the params carry the member identifier `ChainMemberIdentifier`, the deps and the payload carry nothing, and the success return carries the `ChainDeclaration` of the member the identifier names; the function reads each declaration from that member's own module and never from its caller
    * `[✅]`   Functional: for the Base member the function returns `BASE_CHAIN_DECLARATION`, and under the `mocks` feature for the mock member it returns `MOCK_I_CHAIN_ADAPTER_DECLARATION`, each the constant its member owns
